<!-- Updated: count raised to 75 by WP75 — the two items C74's sweep escalated rather than annexed, i.e. the two places where the class lands on the real host rather than on the driver 2026-08-04 -->
**On the count, amended again (2026-08-04) — WP75.** The count is now **75**.

| Step | Count |
|---|---|
| "amended again — WP74" above | 74 |
| **+ WP75** (§5 **C75**) — real-host signal fidelity and gate validity: the constant `applied` and the discarded `session.info` payload | **75** |

**WP75 is not new product scope, and it is the first work package in this run chartered against a defect that hollows a work package already reported as closed.** C74's sweep found six items and deliberately escalated three rather than annexing them; the Dispatcher verified two and chartered them here. Both are the same class C74 named — *"a response field with no reader reads as success"* — but they land on the **real host** rather than on the driver, which is the distinction that makes them a separate component rather than a fifth and sixth criterion on C74. **WP73 is DONE and is not re-opened.** Its guard is structurally correct and every falsification of it was real; all of them, however, used **fake** endpoints that can return `applied: false`, and no real endpoint can — so **C73 AC1 is presently unfalsifiable against the real build, and it becomes meaningful only once WP75 lands.** That relation is recorded as a dependency and a cross-reference, not as a re-opening: WP73's charter was satisfied in full, and an artefact describing it as having failed is factually wrong about a closed WP.

**WP75 is chartered separately rather than folded into WP50, WP73 or WP74 for the reason this run has now used six times.** WP73 is closed and its subject is the driver's *reading* of a flag, not the host's *production* of it. WP74 explicitly excluded `plugin/**` and recorded S1 as escalated, which was correct — a WP74 that widened into the plugin's control surface would have been the unchartered widening five other WPs were held to. WP50 owns the *detector*, and C50 AC5 names the `pluginBuild` requirement without owning the payload it arrives in; splitting one payload's consumption across two work packages is how the field was lost. And **vault distinctness has its own criterion here rather than a line inside C50 AC5 because it is a gate-validity precondition, not a case-level check** — if it fails, no result from the run means anything, including the cases that passed, which is a different category from every other precondition C50 AC5 lists. Same argument that separated WP63 from WP18, WP67 from WP66, WP68 from WP26, WP69 from WP50 and WP73 from WP50.

<!-- Updated: count raised to 82 by WP82 — measured on the two live instances, reported symptom falsified in every particular, defect real and worse than reported 2026-08-05 -->
**On the count, amended again (2026-08-05, B24) — WP82.** The count is now **82**: 81 as below, **+ WP82** — connectivity is measured, not latched.

**WP82 is the second work package in this run whose reported symptom was FALSIFIED by its own rule-12 re-verification and which was chartered anyway, and unlike WP81 the falsification made the defect *worse* rather than re-scoping it.** The report said a peer had silently lost its connection, never reconnected, and logged nothing. Re-measured: **no connection dropped.** Both of vault A's sockets are OPEN and carrying frames — A's own log emitted `[sync] awareness pulse: gap 8011ms (source=message)` five seconds before the charter was drafted, a line reachable only through `tickAwarenessKeepAlive`, whose first statement is `if (this.ws?.readyState !== WebSocket.OPEN) return false` (`sync/sync.ts:655`), and whose `source=message` means an inbound frame drove it. The log is not silent (**128** `[connection]` lines, **34 dated 2026-08-05**). `autoReconnect` did not fail to fire, because **it is not a reconnect driver** — it gates auto-resume at plugin load (`main.ts:513-514`) and neither retry loop reads it. And `roomId` is intact on both peers, which rules out by construction every path that ends a session, so the control channel never reached `disconnected` and the mux never exhausted its attempts.

**What is actually true is one early `return`, and it is a strictly worse defect than an outage.** `plugin.controlConnected` is written `true` at exactly two sites, mutually exclusive by role: `main.ts:985-988`, evaluated at socket-open time under `role === "host"`, and `sync/control-handlers.ts:218`, reachable only past the `role !== "guest"` gate at `:201`. The `join-response` promotion branch `return`s at `:194`. **A peer that resumes as guest and is promoted to host by the relay therefore falls between both gates and is never marked connected again for the life of the session** — pinned by A's own log at millisecond resolution (`02:10:18.784Z resuming as guest` → `.881Z control channel connected` → `.890Z promoted to host`). **And the mirror case, which nobody had named:** vault B resumed *as host*, latched the flag at `:986`, and was then **demoted** — so B's `connected: true` is a latch set on a role B no longer holds. **`connected` is wrong on both peers, in opposite directions, from one defect, and the peer the report used as its healthy control is not one.** An outage is loud and self-limiting; this is silent, permanent, and reported as health.

**Why it is P0 and why it is a §7 concern in its own right.** `updateOnlineState` (`main.ts:191-193`) has run with the latch false, so `FileOpsManager.isOnline` is false and **every file operation vault A performs is being enqueued into an unbounded `OfflineQueue` whose only drain is an edge that can no longer occur** (`file-ops.ts:100-107`, `:74-82`) — while the status bar reads `Live Share: hosting`, because `updateStatusBar` is driven by `connectionState`, a **third** notion of connectivity that never sees the mux at all. Three notions in one process, all three disagreeing on the live host. It is also the reason the canvas E2E suite reads **13/18 rather than 19/19**, which two batches nearly filed as regressions in their own work: **19/19 is not the current baseline until this lands.**

**Two things were deliberately NOT bundled, and the separation is the charter's main structural decision.** The replica divergence is **confirmed as a fact and not attributed** — canvas state travels over the mux, which is open on **both** peers, so it does not follow from the latch, and at least four other causes are live in these vaults this week; WP82 owns *"the peers do not report they have not converged"* and explicitly not *"the peers do not converge"*. And the relay's `clients: 2` **contradicts nothing**: it counts **mux** sockets only (`server/src/index.ts:74-82` → `ws-handler.ts:421-435`), the control WSS is not counted anywhere, and both mux sockets are genuinely alive — recorded as **S35**, because that counter has been read as "peers connected" all week, including in the report that produced this charter. Making `room.hostUserId` stable is `server/**` (S37, 34 role transitions on one vault in 2 h 05 min), an `OfflineQueue` cap is a data-retention decision (S40), and the first-connect outage reported to the user as `Live Share: authentication required` (S39) is named for its own disposition rather than annexed.

<!-- Updated: count raised to 81 by WP80 and WP81 — both found by RUNNING the product, both carried up by the data-loss batch, both previously unowned 2026-08-05 -->
**On the count, amended again (2026-08-05, B20) — WP80 and WP81.** The count is now **81**. The arithmetic is spelled out because the previous step (79, WP79) also had no count paragraph and the header was re-derived from §7 and §9 alone:

| Step | Count |
|---|---|
| "amended again — WP78" below | 78 |
| **+ WP79** — a shared folder mirrors completely on a guest, canvases included (§9 row and the §7 line were both raised at the time; **no count paragraph was written**, which is recorded here rather than left as a silent step) | 79 |
| **+ WP80** — a peer may not purge a manifest it cannot know is complete | 80 |
| **+ WP81** — the log sink says where it is writing, and says once when it cannot | **81** |

**Neither is new product scope, and neither is chartered where it was first suspected.**

**WP80 is chartered separately rather than folded into the data-loss batch because the batch explicitly declined it** (`ImplementationReport_DataLossChain.md` §8 row 2: *"A real fix is 'do not publish a purging manifest until initial sync completes', which is its own work package"*) — and because the two are opposite sides of one wire. The batch closed the **consuming** side: a peer must not delete without positive evidence of authority. WP80 closes the **producing** side: a peer must not assert completeness it cannot have. Folding them would produce one gate where the design needs two independent ones, each failing closed alone. Same argument that separated WP63 from WP18, WP67 from WP66, WP68 from WP26 and WP75 from WP73.

**And the re-verification inverted the batch's own assessment of the residual risk, which is the reason WP80 is not merely defence in depth.** The report records the hazard as *"now bounded on the consuming side"*. Measured against the tree, it is not: a newly-promoted host's truncated purging manifest satisfies **every** condition `cleanupStaleFiles` checks — the attestation is fresh (the promotion advances `seq`), its `hostId` is not the guest's own (so the not-your-own-witness rule passes), a live peer claims host, and the manifest is **short rather than empty** so D3's floor does not fire. The two evidence conditions were built to answer *"did a live host say this?"*, and a live host **did** say it; they cannot answer *"could that host know?"*, because that fact is not on the wire. **WP80 is the one remaining live route from a correct promotion to a destroyed user file, and the D1 repair made promotion work for the first time — so it went from effectively unreachable to routine.**

**WP81 is the first work package in this run whose reported symptom was FALSIFIED by its own rule-12 re-verification and which was chartered anyway, on a re-scoped subject.** The report states the debug log stopped at `2026-08-04T23:56` in both vaults and produced nothing for a day. Read per minute from the two vaults' own log files, it did not: the logs are continuous through that minute and every minute since, and both were still being appended to while the charter was written. The file had **moved** an hour earlier — `H:\tmp\liveshare_fix_debuglog.py` rewrote `debugLogPath` and relocated the file at 22:57Z — and the product said nothing at any point. **The class the report named holds exactly; its mechanism does not.** That distinction is load-bearing: a charter written against the narrative would have specified a repair for a stop that never happened, and its tests would have been green for free — the eleventh instance of the class this run keeps finding, this time avoided by measuring the artefact instead of trusting the report. **The mis-stated timestamp is itself the argument for the WP:** with no instrument, a reader cannot distinguish *"stopped"* from *"moved"*, and this project spent a diagnosis on that difference. WP81 is chartered on what is measurably in the tree — the swallowed rejection on a persistent sink, the view control that mutes the file sink, and the settings fallback that still points at the pre-`7754ac6` location.

**Two items were deliberately NOT chartered and are recorded as unowned rather than annexed.** Making `room.hostUserId` stable is a `server/**` change (`control-handler.ts:588`) and `server/**` is a §7 abort criterion outside WP41 — WP80 makes each election cycle harmless and leaves the churn (**S25**). Bounding the debug log's growth in its new location is a rotation policy, which is a size-management decision with no place inside an observability WP (**S30**). Annexing either would have repeated the unchartered-widening failure five earlier WPs were held to.

<!-- Updated: count raised to 78 by WP78 — the spawning `runner` DEFAULT WP69 landed, which downgraded C45 AC4 from a structural guarantee to a call-site promise and made C71 AC4 unsatisfiable as written 2026-08-04 -->
**On the count, amended again (2026-08-04) — WP78.** The count is now **78**.

| Step | Count |
|---|---|
| "amended again — WP77" below | 77 |
| **+ WP78** — the build runner is required, so no spawn is reachable by accident | **78** |

**WP78 is not new product scope. It is a landed guarantee being restored, and the distinction between "violated" and "no longer guaranteed" is the whole reason it is chartered rather than absorbed.** Three findings, deliberately kept apart because they have different answers.

1. **C45 AC4 is NOT violated today — measured, not assumed.** An AST census of every call to `install.build_e2e_bundle` in the repository returns **22 call sites, 0 bare, 22 supplying `runner=` as a keyword, 0 non-test callers**; all 22 live under `workflowArtifacts/canvas-v2/tests/{visible,blind_set1,blind_set2}/WP69/`. `tools/launch_obsidian_e2e.py` does not import `install` at all. **No long-running process is currently started outside `visible-console`.**
2. **But the guarantee C45 AC4 encoded is gone.** Its value was *structural*: the console is **injected**, therefore no test, no dev loop and no mistaken import can reach the real `Obsidian.exe` or the owner's live working vaults by accident (D16) — C71 §"Why a real spawn backend is the wrong answer" says so in exactly those words, and adds that *"a default-constructed spawn backend converts a structural property into a convention."* **WP69 did precisely that, one module over, and WP69 is `DONE`.** With a spawning **default**, "nobody spawns accidentally" is a property of the call sites — a promise, re-auditable on every future edit. The install step is wired into no entrypoint, so **the first real gate run is exactly the moment someone writes `build_e2e_bundle(plugin_dir)`**; landing WP78 after that is landing it after the only event it protects against.
3. **C71 AC4 was unsatisfiable as written and C71 §3's measurement was stale.** AC4 required a clean grep for `subprocess` / `Popen` / `os.system` / `os.spawn*` under `tools/obsidian_e2e/`, with C71 §6 requiring the report to carry the grep evidence. That grep returns two hits WP71 did not add and may not remove. C71 §3's *"no process spawn anywhere in `tools/obsidian_e2e/` — measured 2026-08-04"* was measured against the WP43–WP49 rig and had already expired when it was written. **§7's own rule, in the flesh: a measurement is not a timeless fact.**

**The ruling — restore the property, do not weaken the criterion.** Amending C71 AC4 to *"no `subprocess` except `install.py`'s"* would edit a landed acceptance criterion until the code satisfied it, which is the move §7 exists to make impossible, and would additionally re-read C45 AC4 as *"through `visible-console`, or by a default nobody is supposed to take"*. Instead the **code** changes: `runner` becomes required, the spawn becomes a named opt-in export, and C71 AC4 is amended into a **reachability property of the parsed package** which is strictly stronger than the text search it replaces — no tree that failed the original passes the amended form. The full argument, with the side-by-side comparison, is recorded at §5 C71 so a reader can check it rather than trust it. **This is the same principle as WP70's and WP77's `Secret` wrapper, which this run has now accepted twice: a type is a guarantee where a call-site audit is only a promise. A required parameter is that guarantee in argument position.**

**The blast radius was measured before the criteria were written, and it is deliberately narrow.** An AST walk finds **42** optional-injectable-with-substituted-default parameter sites in the package; **15** of them substitute a default reaching a real external effect; **exactly one** starts a process. The other fourteen are socket probes, one `urlopen` transport, one room-minting POST, two clocks, two sleepers, an `os.path.isfile`, a ctypes process **lister** and two vault-registry paths — enumerated in the WP78 charter §3 and explicitly **excluded**, because C45 AC4's subject is process *starting* and generalising would be the unchartered widening five WPs in this run were held to. The whole package contains **two** spawn nodes in 9 170 lines (`install.py:101`, `:458`) — and `vaults.default_process_lister` is the landed proof the criterion was understood everywhere else: it uses a ctypes ToolHelp snapshot *"rather than shelling out to `tasklist`/`wmic`, which would start a process and violate AC4."*

**Ordering: WP78 blocks WP71 and nothing else.** It blocks WP71 on **satisfiability**, not compilation — WP71 cannot discharge its own AC4 honestly until this lands, and a WP71 that reported it discharged anyway would be a green that cannot fail in the WP whose subject is preventing exactly that. It does **not** block WP50, WP74, WP75 or WP76: three of those are in another repository and none of the four names a file under `tools/obsidian_e2e/` — measured file overlap **zero**. **One coordination hazard is recorded rather than left to discovery:** WP77 and WP78 both modify `install.py`, in disjoint regions (`BundleState` `:252-264` vs. `_default_runner`/`build_e2e_bundle`/`__all__`), and must not be in flight in the same batch.

<!-- Updated: count raised to 77 by WP77 — the credential-disclosure defect WP70 closed for its own room token and could not reach in the inherited module that holds the OWNER's 2026-08-04 -->
**On the count, amended again (2026-08-04) — WP77.** The count is now **77**.

| Step | Count |
|---|---|
| "amended again — WP76" below | 76 |
| **+ WP77** — the borrowed `data.json` is unrenderable by type, and the byte-exact oracle survives it | **77** |

**WP77 is not new product scope, and it is the second half of a defect WP70 closed at its own boundary.** WP70's tp02 established that a value carrying a credential cannot be rendered by any general-purpose stringification and closed it *by type* for the room token the rig mints. **`tools/obsidian_e2e/ports.py` holds the owner's `data.json` — the file that carries `encryptionPassphrase`, `encryptionSalt`, `jwt`, `serverPassword` and `token` — as raw bytes in a field of a plain `@dataclass`.** A generated `__repr__` prints every field, so `repr`, `str`, an f-string, `%s` in a lazy `logging` call, `dataclasses.asdict` and a failed `pytest` comparison each render the owner's live credentials in full, unconditionally; a traceback frame renders them too wherever locals are captured. Measured headless against the current tree with a synthetic sentinel, with the positive control asserted first — **no owner value was read at any point.** It was carried up rather than fixed in place because `ports.py` is WP44's module and sits outside WP70's declared boundary, which is the same relationship that produced WP63 from WP18, WP67 from WP66, WP68 from WP26, WP69 from WP50 and WP73 from WP50.

**Three findings from the re-verification that the escalation did not contain, each of which changes the shape of the repair.** (1) **The handwritten-repr fix is already landed in this package and already insufficient:** `provisioning._CommunityState` carries `repr=False` plus a hand-written `__repr__`, and `dataclasses.asdict` walks straight past it — the audit-versus-type argument demonstrated on a real attempt, in this repository, by the same WP whose type WP77 reuses. (2) **`ports.py` silently downgrades a WP70 `RedactedMapping` to a plain `dict`** at `provision_port`, so the redaction WP70 built for the minted token is undone by an inherited line one module away — a landed protection lost, not a missing one. (3) **`ports` cannot import `relay`:** `relay` already imports `ports`, so the `Secret` type has to be relocated below both rather than imported across, and a relocation that leaves two `Secret` classes is worse than the leak.

**Ordering: WP77 lands in parallel with the gate WPs and blocks only WP7.** Measured file overlap with every WP on the gate path (`WP70 → WP74 → WP75 → WP76 → WP71 → WP50/51 → WP7`) is **zero** — none of them names a file under `tools/obsidian_e2e/`, and the one that owns that tree, WP70, is `DONE`. There is no rule-10 contract to write and nothing is serialised behind it. **It blocks WP7 absolutely, on execution rather than on compilation:** the leak has never fired, because no gate has run and every borrow state ever constructed has held fixture bytes under `tmp_path`; **WP7 is the first event that points `capture_state` at the owner's two real vaults**, and the run is agent-mediated (C71), so its output reaches a transcript and from there the run's artefacts. §7's data-safety gate already forbids that outcome and makes it an abort criterion for the WP that performs the run. Landing WP77 after WP7 is landing it after the only event it protects against. **No ranking against WP71–WP76 is claimed:** they make the gate's result mean something, this makes the gate safe to run, and the two are orthogonal in the same way WP75 and WP76 are of each other.

<!-- Updated: count raised to 76 by WP76 — the item C75's sweep escalated rather than annexed (S7): the gate's own gesture bypasses the capture path this initiative exists to fix 2026-08-04 -->
**On the count, amended again (2026-08-04) — WP76.** The count is now **76**.

| Step | Count |
|---|---|
| "amended again — WP75" above | 75 |
| **+ WP76** (§5 **C76**) — the gate exercises the capture path, and every case records which path it drove | **76** |

**WP76 is not new product scope, and it is not another instance of the class the last four work packages closed — it is the prior question.** WP73, WP74 and WP75 all ask *"can the gate's result be trusted?"*. WP76 asks *"is the gate running the code the project exists to fix?"*, and the verified answer is **no**. `buildPluginHost.simulateEdit` writes directly into the shared `Y.Doc` (`:996-1005`) and reaches neither capture path, so **the run matrix measures `doc → relay → doc` convergence and nothing else** — it does not establish that a user gesture reaches the doc, nor that a remote delta reaches the view or the file. CONCEPT_V2 Teil 1 Symptom 2 identifies the **capture channel diffing against the wrong base** as the root of the reported corruption cascade, W1 grades it *kritisch*, and I6, the atomic composite registers and the intent diff — the bulk of P0 and P1 — all live on that path. **A gate that injects straight into the `Y.Doc` bypasses every one of them and would go green while proving only that Yjs converges, which was never in doubt.** The gate would validate the transport and say nothing about the fix. That is the justification for the work package, and the driver's own `edit` docstring (`:261`) asserts the opposite in as many words.

**Chartered separately rather than folded into WP50, and this is the seventh time the run has made the argument.** WP50 owns the *per-case verdict semantics* — which oracle decided a case and what its verdict means. WP76 owns *which code path the case exercised at all*, which is a prior and different question: C50's criteria are all decidable against a matrix that never touches capture, and folding this in would put the phase's most consequential gap inside the WP whose failures are hardest to attribute. The gesture's **definition** additionally has to belong to exactly one WP (rule 10): C51 AC2 requires a command that makes the instance save while stale, but C51's change type reaches only `canvas.setFlag`; C53 requires a whole input-layer gesture inventory, in PHASE P5, downstream of the very gate this makes valid. **C76 defines the primitive and C51 composes it, with C51 AC2 unchanged** — the same shape C69 was split out for, where a step named in prose was reached by no acceptance criterion.

<!-- Updated: WP121 and WP122 added by the owner's canvas-convergence ruling; the narrow option was chosen and the broad one declined 2026-08-08 -->
**Amended (2026-08-08) — WP121 and WP122, the canvas convergence remodel. NO TOTAL IS RESTATED, and that is deliberate.**

| WP | Phase | Title | Scope summary | Depends on | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WP121 | P1 | Winning is not a licence to discard | a conflict copy — or a refusal — on the canvas `doc-wins` arm of `coldOpen`, on a **record-level** predicate; the ledger gains a canvas arm | — | planned |
| WP122 | P1 | A file with no writer cannot converge | bind the CRDT→disk writer on the **host's own creates**: a fourth mirror verdict (R2), the host arm of `mirrorOne` returning it (R1), and `armCanvasMirrorPass()` from the create coordinator's accepted branch (R5 / `S170`) | **WP121** | planned |

**Dependency order is 121 → 122 and it is hard.** `attachCanvasWriter` → `attachCanvasPersistence` → `coldOpen()`, and a cold open on a non-empty doc takes the `doc-wins` branch and **flushes** (`canvas-persistence.ts:608-620`). WP122 therefore converts a path that is today reachable only when a human opens a board into one that fires automatically, on every host-held canvas in the manifest, at the next mirror pass — **on boards that are already divergent today, all of them at once.** WP121 is not a safety net bolted on afterwards; it is the precondition that makes WP122's attach legal.

**The owner's decision, recorded because the alternatives were live options and were declined.** Three ways out of the remodel were put to the owner on 2026-08-08 (`DISPATCHER_STATE.md` §9): *(i)* let the host's file follow like any peer; *(ii)* keep the pass hands-off and guarantee convergence through the live writer; *(iii)* canonicalise serialisation everywhere so all copies match at birth. **The owner chose the narrow form of (ii) — bind the writer on the host's own creates — and explicitly declined (i).** The broad reading is not chartered here and must not be reached for by an implementor who finds the narrow one awkward. The second question put with it — **canvas-only versus the general file-sync model — was DEFERRED**, so both packages are scoped to `.canvas` and neither touches the `.md` path, which is measured unaffected (a guest's editor edit reaches the host's disk at the first poll, 0.00 s).

**Neither WP is new product scope.** WP122 closes the top item on the §3 measured-unfixed queue and the one that violates the owner's priority-1 ruling directly: a guest's edit reaches a **host-created** canvas's document in seconds and its **file** never — unchanged after 240 s and after four unrelated manifest changes, then converged in 0.00 s the instant a human opened the board, while a **guest**-created board takes 0.25 s because its create handshake already attaches the writer. WP121 closes the `doc-wins` path that WP122 arms.

**WP121 is chartered separately rather than folded into WP122, and the argument is this spec's standard one, with one addition.** The two are different boundaries (the file write versus the writer's existence) and different invariants (I11 versus full convergence), and **folding a safety net into the WP whose behaviour it catches would leave the net untested by construction** — the argument that separated WP63 from WP18, WP67 from WP66, WP68 from WP26 and WP69 from WP50. The addition is sequencing: WP121 must be **demonstrably green before WP122 lands**, not merely in the same commit, because WP122's blast radius is every already-divergent board on every peer at the next session start.

⚠ **WP121 is NOT chartered on a demonstrated data loss, and the record must not be read as if it were.** `DISPATCHER_STATE.md` §6 **withdraws** the claim that opening a canvas destroyed 318 nodes of the owner's board (the board is healthy at 9 nodes / 2 247 B on all three vaults; the 327-node snapshot was our own test spam; the change is not attributable to `doc-wins`), and §4 files this path under *"traced in code, NOT measured"*. `Investigation_CanvasConvergence.md` §6 is **superseded on that point**. WP121's first acceptance criterion is therefore the demonstration itself, and a failure to produce one is recorded as a result rather than papered over.

**No total is restated, and the omission is reported rather than hidden.** The count paragraphs in this section stop at **76**, but WP79 and WP85–WP120 were chartered without §9 rows. Writing "the count is now 78" would be arithmetic over a table that is missing twenty packages, which is the kind of true-sounding number this project has already been bitten by (`S166`). The two rows above are entered; **repairing the §9 backlog is a separate Dispatcher item and is not claimed here.**

<!-- Updated: WP7 gains WP74 and WP75 — WP74's own §9 dependency amendment was never written, and is entered here rather than back-dated 2026-08-04 -->
**Amended dependencies (2026-08-04, second) — and one omission corrected.** One row, one deliberate non-dependency, and one correction to the previous amendment block:

| WP | Phase | Title | Depends on (was) | Depends on (now) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| WP7 | P0 | E2E rig as mandatory gate | WP4, WP5, WP50, WP51, WP69, WP70, WP71, WP72, WP73 | WP4, WP5, WP50, WP51, WP69, WP70, WP71, WP72, WP73, **WP74, WP75** |

**Correction, stated rather than back-dated: WP74's own dependency amendment was never entered.** `TaskCharter_WP74` §2 states *"WP7 depends on this WP"* and C74 states that it *"must land before the gate run"*, but no §9 row was written for it, so the table above carried WP7's dependencies only as far as WP73. The row is entered here, covering WP74 and WP75 together, rather than by editing the 2026-08-04 block above — an amendment block is a record of when a decision was taken and is not rewritten after the fact (rule 5, applied to the spec's own registers).

**WP75 blocks WP7 and deliberately does NOT block WP50, and the argument is the one this spec has already made four times — with one difference.** WP50 is the *detector* charter, verified against **fake** endpoints; the plugin-side half of WP75 is a TypeScript repair in a different repository, and making WP50 wait on it would serialise a Python driver behind it for no verification gain. The driver-side half is in WP50's own file, which is why **WP75 depends on WP50 rather than the reverse**: WP50 restructures that file and a repair landed underneath it would have to be re-derived. **The difference is in how strongly it blocks WP7.** WP73 and WP74 block the run because a specific false green becomes reachable without them — they make the gate *able to detect* things. WP75 blocks it because without it a green WP7 would carry a falsifiability property it does not have (Part A: the guard its per-case verdict depends on is a check on a constant) and would be compatible with **one vault syncing with itself over a build nobody identified** (Part B). That is not a weakened gate but a vacuous one, and it is indistinguishable from a real green in the artefact. **WP75 makes the gate's result mean anything at all.** Whether WP51 or WP52 should also wait is not decided here and is not claimed.

<!-- Updated: WP7 gains WP76, and one consequence for WP51 is recorded rather than back-dated 2026-08-04 -->
**Amended dependencies (2026-08-04, third) — WP76.** One row, one deliberate non-dependency, and one consequence left to the Dispatcher:

| WP | Phase | Title | Depends on (was) | Depends on (now) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| WP7 | P0 | E2E rig as mandatory gate | WP4, WP5, WP50, WP51, WP69, WP70, WP71, WP72, WP73, WP74, WP75 | WP4, WP5, WP50, WP51, WP69, WP70, WP71, WP72, WP73, WP74, WP75, **WP76** |

**WP76 blocks WP7, does NOT block WP50, and is NOT ordered against WP75 — the last of those is a correction to how the finding was first framed.** It blocks WP7 absolutely: without it a green WP7 is a true statement about `doc → relay → doc` convergence *presented as* a statement about the capture path, and the driver's own docstring already asserts the latter, so the artefact would be indistinguishable from a real green to any reader who trusted the tool's description of itself. It does not block WP50 for exactly the reason WP75 did not — WP50 is the *detector* charter, verified against **fake** endpoints, and every C50 criterion stays decidable against injectable seams; **the relation is WP76 → depends on → WP50**, because Part B lands in WP50's own file. **But C50 AC1's file-level oracle becomes meaningful only once WP76 lands**, the same "becomes meaningful when" form C75 used for C73 AC1 and for the same reason: `attachCanvasWriter` is reachable only from the two `main.ts` wiring sites, so a canvas the rig subscribed and never opened has no disk writer, and `sameFileObservation` returns `true` for two absent files. That is a cross-reference and a warning to WP50's implementor, not a re-opening.

**On the proposed ranking of WP76 above WP75: rejected, and the reason is recorded because the framing is otherwise persuasive.** The argument offered was *"a gate that runs the wrong path cannot be rescued by making its signals honest"* — true, and the converse is equally true: a gate that runs the **right** path while unable to tell two vaults from one, over a build nobody identified, with a constant `applied`, is also unrescuable. **The two are orthogonal failures of the same run, not a ranking:** WP75 makes the run's *signals* true, WP76 makes the run's *subject* the right one, and each is independently sufficient to void WP7. The one asymmetry worth recording cuts the other way and concerns the **artefact**: a WP76-less gate whose docstring has been corrected is *honestly labelled* — it says it measures transport, and it does — whereas a WP75-less gate lies about whether it ran at all. **Sequencing therefore follows from the files, not from the ranking:** WP76 lands after WP75 because both edit `simulateEdit`'s module and `run_matrix`, because WP76's new gesture inherits C75 AC1's "report whether it happened rather than whether the command was accepted" discipline verbatim, and because a WP76 gesture returning a literal would re-create in a new command the exact defect WP75 removes from the old one.

**Consequence for WP51, recorded and NOT enacted.** Under C76's one-definer ruling, **WP51 depends on WP76** — C51 AC2 composes the gesture C76 defines. `TaskCharter_WP51` is `planned` and its own row does not carry WP76, and back-dating it is what rule 5 forbids, so the relation is entered here as a **Dispatcher decision**. If it is ruled the other way — C51 defines the gesture and C76 consumes it — then WP51 must land before both WP76 and WP7, which reverses two existing dependency rows and requires C51's declared change type (today `canvas.setFlag` and its runtime-flag map) to be widened to a new command family. Whether WP52 should also wait, and whether any part of C53 should be pulled forward, are not decided here and are not claimed.

**WP71 is a missing chartered capability, not a failure of WP43–WP49, and the distinction is load-bearing.** Those seven WPs delivered exactly what they were chartered to deliver — discovery, port provisioning, launch planning, readiness, scratch safety, teardown and the oracles are all real. **C45 AC4 forbids the rig from starting a process outside `visible-console`**, and the rig has no MCP client, so `PlanOnlyConsole` is the correct consequence of that criterion rather than an unfinished implementation of something else. What no charter ever reached is the **mediation**: the artefact that carries a whole run's plan, the feedback of each step's outcome, and the statement that the executed sequence was the planned one. Without it, C7 AC1's *"executes end to end"* has no satisfiable reading, and §7's requirement of *"a recorded, reproducible green run plus a documented invocation"* cannot be met by any command that exists. WP71 is chartered separately rather than written as procedure text in WP7 because a procedure has no acceptance criterion and nothing can falsify "the agent followed it"; reproducibility has to be carried by an artefact. The alternative — giving the rig a real spawn backend — was **evaluated and rejected**, with the argument recorded in full at C71: it contradicts a landed AC, it converts the injected-console structural guarantee protecting the owner's live vaults into a convention, and it would still leave the plan/outcome artefact necessary while hiding which steps had been improvised.

**WP72 and WP73 are live defects found by measurement, and each is chartered separately for the reason this run has now used five times.** WP72's borrow-clobber (`setFlag` → `saveSettings()` → the `data.json` C70 borrows) is named by **no** existing charter; C51 AC3 owns only the rejection of inert flags, and folding the file-write behaviour into it would leave the clobber with no criterion of its own. WP73 is the **seventh** instance in this run of *a green test that cannot fail*, and the first located in the instrument that renders the release gate's own per-case verdicts: C50 AC3 already forbids reporting a case whose gesture did not take effect as a pass, and the driver cannot honour it because it throws the `applied` flag away at all **eleven** `_simulate` call sites. **The eleven is measured and corrects a "six" in the original report** — six is the number of matrix cases, not of call sites, and a repair scoped to six would have left five gestures unchecked. That correction is itself an instance of rule 12.

<!-- Updated: WP7 gains WP71, WP72 and WP73 — the run cannot be mediated, its control surface can destroy the borrow its verdict is checked against, and its driver cannot detect an unapplied gesture 2026-08-04 -->
**Amended dependencies (2026-08-04).** One row, and one deliberate non-dependency:

| WP | Phase | Title | Depends on (was) | Depends on (now) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| WP7 | P0 | E2E rig as mandatory gate | WP4, WP5, WP50, WP51, WP69, WP70 | WP4, WP5, WP50, WP51, WP69, WP70, **WP71, WP72, WP73** |

**WP71, WP72 and WP73 deliberately do NOT depend on each other**, and none of them depends on WP69 or WP70. WP71 is verified against a fixture plan and a fake console seam; WP72 under the `plugin/` vitest gate at the control-surface boundary; WP73 against fake endpoints that return `applied: false` on demand. **WP7 is again where every one of these dependencies is real:** it is the run, and it is the only place where an unmediated launch, a clobbered borrow or an undetected unapplied gesture turns a green case into a false pass.

**WP70 is not new product scope, and it is the second half of the same discovery that produced WP69.** The pre-flight established that the gate had no way to *build* the bundle it needs (WP69). Reading the gate's own preconditions further shows it also had no way to *connect* the two instances it builds that bundle for: WP44 provisions exactly one settings key, WP47 places the scratch canvas in a rig-owned folder, and **nothing established that the folder is inside the shared surface** — so the gate could converge on a file neither instance was syncing and report it as a pass. That is the vacuous-green class this whole run exists to eliminate, sitting on the release gate itself. In the same place, WP7 §2 states that the gate "runs against a local relay" while nothing in `tools/obsidian_e2e/` starts one and no rig module reads `serverUrl`.

**It is chartered separately rather than folded into WP44 or WP50 for three reasons.** WP44 is `DONE` and its subject is one key and a byte-exact borrow; re-opening it to provision nine more would put a new blast radius inside a landed WP whose ACs never reached it. WP50 owns the *detector* (C50 AC5) and must stay independent of the *provisioner* — a detector its own provisioner may satisfy trivially is worthless, and merging them is the one change that would make C50 AC5 unfalsifiable. And the relay is a process lifecycle with its own port, its own store paths and its own release criterion, which is a different layer from a verdict-rendering driver: §9 records that PHASE T3 was decomposed fine-grained precisely so a failure localises to one layer rather than to "the rig". This is the same argument that separated WP63 from WP18, WP67 from WP66, WP68 from WP26 and WP69 from WP50.

**WP69 is not new product scope either, and it is not a convenience.** It is a precondition the spec has always assumed and never owned: `T3_SharedContract` §1.1 states in prose that "a real run requires a **dev build** installed into the vaults" and assigns that step to "WP50/WP51" — but neither charter's scope, acceptance criteria or Definition-of-Done artefacts ever contained it, so the step had a pointer and no owner. The 2026-08-02 pre-flight then measured why it cannot simply be done in passing: the only build that sets `__LS_E2E__` true is the watch build, which never terminates, so there is no supported way to *produce* the bundle the gate needs, let alone install it. It is chartered separately rather than added to WP50 because it is the only work in this phase that edits a file whose output **ships to users**, and because its central criterion — the production bundle is byte-identical before and after — is not a statement about the run matrix at all. See the C69 note in §5 for the full argument, including why the fire-and-forget-and-kill-the-watcher alternative was rejected.

**WP63 is deliberately NOT a dependency of WP19–WP23.** It depends on WP18 and must be DONE before P1 closes, but nothing in the tombstone wiring, the quarantine auditor, the two removals or the fuzzer needs it in place first. This ordering is intentional: the P1 queue must not be serialised behind it.

**On the count, amended again (2026-08-01).** The count is now **58**. The four added WPs (§5 PHASE VI) are not new product scope — they repair the mechanism that was supposed to be verifying the other 54, and re-establish which of its historical verdicts were real. They are chartered separately rather than folded into a maintenance pass because one of them (WP57) can invalidate closed handovers, which is a finding the project must be able to cite by artefact.

**On the count, amended (2026-08-01).** The count is now **54**. The twelve added WPs are the T3 host layer (§5 PHASE T3). They were not an oversight in the original decomposition: the original spec assumed `tools/launch_liveshare_e2e.py` was the rig CONCEPT_V2 Teil 14 refers to, and it is not — it is a headless mock-host rig that scopes real-Obsidian orchestration out by design. WP7 was therefore chartered against a rig that did not exist, and Worker 3 correctly refused to run the substitute. The twelve WPs are the honest cost of the gate the concept always specified; they are fine-grained because a real-Obsidian run is slow and stateful, so a failure must localise to one layer (discovery, provisioning, launch, identity, safety, teardown, oracle, driver) rather than to "the rig".

---

## 10. Operational Rules

- **Logging — the declared signature set after V2.** Every signature has exactly one production emitter.

| Signature | Status | Owner |
|---|---|---|
| `SCATTER:` / `DETACH:` / `NO TYPE:` | kept | quarantine auditor (WP20) |
| `LOCK REVERT:` | kept | `main.ts:revertCanvasNode` |
| `AWARENESS GAP:` | kept | `sync.ts` |
| `DRAG WATCHDOG:` / `ADAPTER PATCH:` | kept | `canvas-adapter.ts` |
| `CANVAS WRITER:` | kept | `canvas-persistence.ts` |
| `LOCK DENIED:` | **retired** | removed with WP21 |
| `CANVAS TEXT FALLBACK:` | **retired** | removed with WP33 |
| `SHADOW STALE:` | new | WP4 — a discarded staleness observation |
| `QUARANTINE:` | new | WP20 — quarantine set / released |
| `INGEST REJECTED:` | new | WP18 — a local record refused at a write boundary, with boundary and reason |
| `SEED REFUSED:` / `SEED RESTORED:` | new | WP63 — file write withheld after a seed refusal / withhold lifted |
| `SEED REFUSAL STORE:` | new | WP90 — the durable refused set: hydration, quarantine of an unreadable store, and a failed write |
| `MUTE OVERRUN:` | new | WP93 — `file-ops.ts`, one emitter. `MUTE OVERRUN: <pathClass> held=<n>ms ceiling=<n>ms (overruns=<n>)`. **Path CLASS only, never the path.** Named *overrun* rather than *clamp*: the emitter can establish that its bound was exceeded; attributing that to a host clamp is an inference it cannot make. Carries state-readable per-class counters, because under S65/S71 the log flushes at ~0.5 s normally and 60 s under a clamp. |
| `DELETE WITHHELD:` | new | WP94 — `canvas-sync.ts`. Reason set `no-open-surface \| no-receipt \| incomplete-observation \| refused-at-ingest`, **plus state-readable per-reason counters**. Deliberately **not** a seventh `CAPTURE DECLINED:` reason: those six describe a whole pass that captured nothing and fire *before* the diff, whereas this is **per-record inside a pass that did capture** — folding it in would break that set's closure and destroy the `modify`-vs-`declines` correlation used to characterise S78. Counters are mandatory because the log flushes at ~0.5 s normally and **60 s under a host clamp** (S65/S71). |
| `CAPTURE DECLINED:` | new | WP91 — `canvas-sync.ts` `handleLocalModify`. `CAPTURE DECLINED: <path> reason=<echo\|not-subscribed\|read-only\|schema-major\|no-doc\|no-file> (declines=<n>)`. Path and reason only. **Declined**, not *dropped*: after WP91 there is no drop, only a decision that names itself. |
| `SIDECAR:` | new | WP24/WP25 — load, compaction, degradation |
| `EPOCH CONFLICT:` | new | WP28 — with both epoch values |
| `MODE:` | new | WP31/WP32 — mode announcement and degradation |

- **Monitoring:** the debug ring buffer plus the optional file sink remain the only observation channel; no telemetry is added. No per-frame logging in hot paths.
- **Recovery / backups:** the sidecar is the client-side recovery path; the conflict copy (`<name>.conflict-<date>.canvas`) is the user-visible archive; the relay blob store (P6) is the room-side recovery path. None of the three is a prerequisite of another.
- **Security and access rules:** the relay stays content-blind — P6 stores opaque frames and must not parse or decrypt them. No secret ever passes through an agent tool or a command string. `SERVER_PASSWORD`, `docker/.env` and the deploy stack are out of scope and must not be read. The protected NA infrastructure (`neural-angels-access`, `n8n`) is never touched.
- **Dependency policy:** zero new runtime dependencies (D11). Any dependency change requires a publish date ≥7 days old, verified with `npm view <pkg>@<version> time.created`. `npm ci` in all build/deploy contexts; `npm install` only for a deliberate local add.
- <!-- Updated: process finding from B3 — a red tree could not be handed back green because uncommitted earlier batches shared the same files 2026-08-02 --> **Snapshot discipline before the first coder attempt (mandatory).** A batch that modifies a file already carrying **uncommitted** work from an earlier batch must snapshot that file before the first coder attempt (a copy under `workflowArtifacts/canvas-v2/_snapshots/<batch>/`, not a `git stash`). B3 could not restore a green baseline because `canvas-sync.ts` and `canvas-persistence.ts` carried B2's WP16/WP17 work and B3's WP18 work in one unversioned diff, with no pre-WP18 point between them — a `git checkout` would have destroyed B2. Reverting was correctly judged more dangerous than reporting honestly, but the choice should never have been forced. **The ability to hand back a green tree is a deliverable, not a courtesy**, and it costs one file copy.
- **Build output:** `plugin/main.js` and `server/dist/` are committed build artifacts — never hand-edited, always regenerated with `npm run build`. ~~`plugin/manifest.json` is a broken symlink; the real manifest is the repo-root one. Do not read or edit the symlink.~~ **No longer true — corrected 2026-08-07.** It was git mode `120000` pointing at `/home/mewski/Projects/obsidian-live-share/manifest.json`, so on Windows it checked out as a 55-byte text file and **the repo could not produce an installable plugin folder**. It is now a real file at mode `100644` (`083fbe0` fixed the content, `67f1036` the mode — the first commit alone left a symlink whose target had become the JSON document, which looks correct in every diff). **Read and edit it normally**; it is the manifest that ships beside `main.js`.
